A PISA study has also shown that the best 20 percent of the third performance group in a primary school are as good as the worst 20 percent of a AHS. The crucial point, however, is: One sit in the AHS, and the other sit in the primary school, and the paths differentiate. There we come to the area of social inequality and injustice, because it is precisely social origin that determines much more than the opportunities in the children themselves. I can say it from my own experience: I'm a classic worker child. My best friend in folk school had the same grades as me. She was the daughter of a lawyer. It was perfectly clear that she was going to the gymnasium. My parents said: Let's see how she develops, she should go to primary school once! I would like to comment briefly on three points: first on the novel of the Federal Theatre Organisation Act, second on the Federal Theatre Evaluation in principle, and then on the issue of the publication of the reports in particular. [[President Dr. Graf takes over the chairmanship.]] I can begin with the novelle on the Federal Theatre Organisation Act and join with Mr Zinggl's speech immediately. Yes, I am a modern, effective public administration, but it is also a great concern to me that we talk about the individual areas of responsibility again and again and again about who is responsible for what function and role and, therefore, what degree of information and knowledge is needed for his responsibility. For me, these are the key elements, if you want to, of public governance. Not everything that applies to the business sector must also be practical for the public sector. That is why we have to evaluate each individual case, I think, exactly from the point of view of public and public interest. The novelle to the Bundestheaterorganisationsgesetz has essentially five individual objectives. First point: It is about the Supervisory Board's catalogue of competence to clarify the duties of the Supervisory Boards and, above all, the consented business. That is to say, creating a clear, comprehensive, uniform catalogue of supervisory councils in accordance with the GesmbH law was a goal and mission here. Second point: Recast of the appointment of Supervisory Boards. This is also a question of clarity. Mr Zinggl, the function of the Supervisory Board is to exercise control in the interests of the owner. The Federal Ministry of Education, Art and Culture is responsible for ownership. [[Abg. Dr. Zinggl: Owner is the Republic!]] I am the one who also has the political responsibility to bear. It is therefore obvious that supervisory councils must also enjoy the trust of the owner's representative. Who finally has political responsibility if something does not work? - Then that's in my field of competence. And that is why this provision is important, analogous to the provisions of social law. Third point: Clearance of the right of conduct. We have anchored in Austria the freedom of art in the constitutional rank, which is a central and important point. Let's just see what's happening in Hungary right now! This is a high value in Austria, and it is also important to me that the artistic directors of the theatre, the museums are in place accordingly and that the right to conduct is also documented accordingly. [[Abg. Grosz: What happens in Hungary?]] The fourth point is the public discussion already mentioned. There should be public audience discussions, twice per season, with appropriate protocol and treatment in the Supervisory Board. The fifth point concerns the change of name in the law: Theatre service in “ART for ART”. This corresponds to the brand name, as it is already in use. These are the points on the innovation of the Federal Theatre Organisation Act. I am pleased if there is broad agreement here. Second point: Bundestheater evaluation. Against the background of the budgetary situation, especially in the light of the cultural policy debate that has now begun over a somewhat questionable book in Germany, it is important that we do everything we can to ensure that the funds for art and culture are used as effectively as possible – just as we are working intensively in the field of education that the funds also arrive in the classroom, with the students. That was the motive for the evaluation. I am now very, very happy that we have done this evaluation. Finally, it was a mission from the government programme to me, and I would like to thank all those involved, especially the government partner, ad personam Mrs Fuhrmann, very much for the cooperation! This evaluation was carried out on the basis of the government programme, and the results, the detailed results with all the data and facts, were recorded in external opinions, in evaluation reports. These evaluation reports, ladies and gentlemen, also include data and information on business secrets of the Bundestheater. I do not have to carry out here, I believe, further that the Bundestheater is in competition as a distributed company – in competition with other stages, opera houses, theatres – and there are legitimate interests of the Bundestheater, which can also be protected accordingly. More focused on the artistic programme. It is very important to us that we continue to pay attention to the fact that art and culture are not solely assessed according to market economy criteria. Not only must the representation of art and culture be diverse, but also access to art and culture must be possible for all regardless of social status. That is why we also stand for an admission price fixation, so that lettuce quotas are available. For several years the ticket prices have not been increased; in this season there has been an increase in ticket prices. In the Burgtheater for play they move between 5 € and 51 €, the pitches cost 2.50 €. Subscribers have the advantage that the increase will not take place until the next season. In conclusion: We are partners of the artists in Austria, and we support them in presenting their creativity, their ability to present them publicly, and therefore we cannot allow existing representation possibilities, as some neoliberal circles or economists demand, to be closed. It's very concrete: With us there will certainly be no closing of playgrounds such as the Academy Theatre! Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-03-29-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00150_d8e12164	Mrs Bundesminister Dr. Karl spoke on a point of view. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-03-29-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00150_d8e12188	Mr President. High house! I am very pleased to have heard so many positive contributions to this presentation by the Ministry of Justice, both in the Committee on Justice and today in plenary. This government template is already based on very extensive and years of negotiations that have begun on the issue of the very sensitive data protection admissibility of the transmission of health data. As early as 2010, the ministerial draft was reviewed. However, the discussions that followed were very difficult. It was not until the summer of 2011 that a compromise was reached with all stakeholders and with further negotiations between the insurance industry, data and consumer protection, the Medical Chamber and the Ministry of Health. At this point, I would like to thank all the relevant employees of my house, first and foremost Mr. Georg Kathrein, for the special commitment to the coming of this law. [[Congratulations with the ÖVP and representatives of the SPÖ.]] My Dear Ones Ladies and gentlemen, this project is based on three pillars. For the first time, it creates clear legal basis for electronic communications between the insurer and its customers. We are trying to avoid legal and economic disadvantages for policyholders in the use of new communications technologies. As a condition for this part, it was necessary to examine the insurance contract law as a whole where the law requires writtenness for declarations, advertisements and communications. For the most part, these regulations were then amended by allowing, in addition to the written form, the written form, also by fax or e-mail, which is probably much more similar to the reality of life today. Another focus is the data protection regulations that have already been discussed on several occasions. In general, the health data should only be transmitted with prior consent of the policyholder. The requirements for this approval will now be strengthened. There should be the possibility that the policyholder will be informed beforehand and that there will also be the possibility to object to the concrete transmission. In the case of direct compensation in sickness insurance, certain data important for the calculation can be determined by law. But, of course, the person concerned has a right of objection. In addition, we limit the types of data that may be determined by law. That is, the insurer should only get those data that are really needed for the billing, and no data that goes beyond that. Thirdly, the law also improves consumer protection and, in general, the policyholder. This key objective has already been supported by the rules on electronic communications which have already been discussed. In addition, this bill provides for a general right of withdrawal, and the rules on the so-called early retirement for life insurance are also strengthened. As early as 2006, the legislator established rules that limit the financial losses of policyholders in the event of early termination of the contract. These provisions are now also intended to cover: the so-called net policy in which the policyholder pays the commission directly to the broker or another intermediary. Finally, these regulations are supplemented by a change in the broker's law. In other words, the insurance broker should only be obliged to carry out a solvency check in the future if this is also necessary in the individual case. It is about the solvency audit of the insurance company he provides. It is not necessary if the insurer is subject to a financial market surveillance compliance with the European standard. High house! You see, therefore, that this proposal contains a number of advantages for policyholders and therefore, of course, for most of the citizens of Austria. I therefore hope for very broad approval. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-03-29-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00150_d8e14330	Madam President. High house! I have taken action to pursue an appropriate judicial policy, which, of course, does not close public discussions and is of course open to criticism. As a convinced Democrat, however, I also believe that the House should be the right place for in-depth discussions on legislative proposals and, in my opinion, should even be. I am therefore not afraid to draw up ideas and criticisms in this legislative decision-making process so that we can come to a better solution together. I would also like to point out, however, that this criticism of a very important, of course, but still of a detailed question of the draft law now before us. The main points of this paper have actually remained uncommented. They have now been stressed by Mr Fazekas, and I would therefore like to take these very important points very briefly before addressing the criticisms mentioned. Freedom of travel and movement between the European Member States has increased, and this, of course, has an impact on the various areas. It is therefore also important for me to speed up and improve the exchange of information from the criminal register, namely the exchange of information from the criminal register between the various EU Member States. In order to prevent pre-punities from coming from another EU Member State from being disregarded in the event of a conviction in Austria. Also in the future, the prohibitions on activities of a minor in connection with sexual offences in the EU foreign country must be entered in the criminal register. In order to resolve existing legal gaps in my policy of protecting children from violence and sexual abuse, youth welfare providers will in future be able to obtain an unlimited query of the criminal record of a potential violent offender. The aim is to better assess the specific suspicion of child welfare and, of course, to avoid and prevent possible abuses. This also corresponds to a call from the national conference. In addition, youth welfare providers should be given the opportunity to obtain information from the sexual offender file to assess the suitability of care and adoption parents. Finally, experience in economic criminal proceedings - and I now come to the subject of criticism - has shown that the long duration of the proceedings depends on the duration of the decision whether or not certain documents may be consulted. Therefore, we have been looking for measures to simplify and accelerate the procedure on the basis of an objection from the person concerned to ensuring that documents are secured without loss of the rule of law, without jeopardising legal protection positions. That is to say, on the one hand, I have repeatedly faced the criticism that the procedures take too long, on the other hand, the measures for acceleration and discharge as an attack on fundamental freedoms have also been criticised. My goal was then to find a good, satisfactory solution out of this double mill. After consulting experts, we finally reached a good solution in the judicial committee. And that, Mr Grosz, was certainly not a justice according to the motto “Wish what!” but really a good solution, with the help of the judges of the parliamentary groups – except for the BZÖ – and with the addition of experts. This solution, which we were able to achieve, maintains the fundamental separation between the conduct of the proceedings by the public prosecutor and the control by the court, but the procedure for ensuring secure documents is regulated more clearly and transparently. Finally, the professional secrecy of those professional groups whose right of refusal is already recognised by a prohibition of circumvention threatened with invalidity is to be fully protected. I thank you for the constructive spirit in the negotiations of the Committee on Justice, which in the end makes it really possible to find a very good solution together. I therefore also welcome the fact that an amendment to the current legal situation makes it absolutely clear that this protection of professional secrets and thus the trust of those concerned is maintained even if the professional secrecy person concerned is himself accused of the act. Let me refer to the link between the opinion of the Oberlandesgericht Graz and § 112 StPO. An amendment to § 112 StPO was foreseen in the assessment draft, and this amendment was criticised by OLG Graz. This was taken as an occasion by my employees in the house to look at this § 112 StPO again and to make further changes, precisely in the context of speeding up the procedure, especially in the major economic criminal proceedings, and in the context of the fact that this provision is also better designed, more clearly designed, more unmistakable. We have not – thank God, Mr Steinhauser – returned to the old legal situation, but we – and I think this is very important – have made this regulation more clear, and we have also put in place things that are very common in practice, for example, that the judge also contributes to the view, which in practice is usually so handled, but not legally anchored. This point has now been legitimised, and I believe this is very important in the sense of legal certainty. That is why this is even an improvement in comparison to the old regulation, which is very welcome. But I would also like to make one point, Mr Grosz. They have said that an honorable judge should again be lord of the preliminary proceedings. To this end, I would like to say that the prosecutors are also honorable persons who do very good work. - Thank you.

[[15:47:10]] 10. Point Report of the Justice Committee on the Government Template (1650, e.g._Other Organiser Federal Law on the obligation to submit an energy card on sale and on the in-consistency gift of buildings and objects of use (Energy ID template law 2012 – EAVG 2012) (1701 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2012-03-29-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00150_d8e14880	We're on our way to the 10th. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. First, Mr Mag. Mag. Stefan speaking. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-03-29-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00150_d8e14907	Madam President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! The Energy ID Template Act is a typical example of unnecessary premeditation as established by the European Union in compliance with ÖVP and SPÖ. What is the background to this law? – There is already an energy ID template law. The regulation is now being adopted in such a way as to: One can have such an energy card, i.e. an opinion on the energy efficiency of his property, his home and so on. If you have such a thing, it will be passed on to the buyer or tenant in the course of the purchase contract or contract, and this will be considered as the basis of the contract. If there is no such thing, then simply one of the types and method of construction of this object is considered to be an agreed energy efficiency, and the seller is liable for it. This is a clear rule. If I as a property owner want the security that an expert, i.e. a card holder, is liable for it, then I will get such an opinion, will pay something for it and will present it. If I have the impression that this is not necessary because the property anyway corresponds to the state of the art or the state of the construction, then I will not have to do so. The new regulation now states: I must have such an opinion drawn up, such an energy ID, and I must definitely present it. If I don't, then I immediately have to pay an administrative penalty up to €1 420. That's the result, and I don't understand the meaning. Because when it comes to making us more sensitive in the field of energy saving measures and so on, it has no effect at all, because it does not improve the object, it has no effect whatsoever on what is in it, but it is only the expert. If there's inside, the object has an enormously high consumption, then I can hand it over to the buyer, and he knows it is. If the consumption is lower, then he has been lucky, and if it is not, the expert is liable for it. So this is nothing at all that will lead to the improvement of environmental protection, but it is only a further advance of the citizens. Costs are reduced because everyone now has to have such an opinion, and it can always lead to punishment. These are exactly the measures I simply do not understand where I do not understand why there is no need to intervene in the freedom of the citizens' treaty, why there is no need to intervene in their own reason. That is why we definitely reject it.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-03-29-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00150_d8e15310	Madam President. High house! With this proposal, we are implementing a directive of the European Union, the so-called Building Directive 2010. The aim of this Directive is to reduce energy consumption in the building sector in particular and to reduce the European Union's energy dependence and greenhouse gas emissions by increasing the use of renewable energy. The existing instruments are to be refined and expanded, and the regulatory will simply be more effective. The rules on the production, presentation and suspension of the energy ID have remained essentially the same, but have been modified and improved in detail. Compared to the applicable law, the draft, as one of the most important innovations, contains a special information requirement when switching real estate inserates in newspapers or in electronic media, with two important energy parameters, namely heating heat demand and the overall energy efficiency factor. The provisions on exemptions from the obligations of the Energy ID Template Act for certain categories of buildings are now to be made uniformly throughout the country without reference to the country regulations. As a legal consequence of the identity card, the seller or inventory holder shall be liable to the buyer or consignor for the correctness of the energy card as well as for the direct liability of the card holder to the buyer or consignor. However, the assessment of this accuracy should take into account bandwidths which are simply inevitable for technical reasons in determining the energy characteristics. In the event of a failure to issue the energy card after the conclusion of the contract, the buyer or the consignor may, at the request of no results, either request the issue of the ID card in court or obtain an energy card himself and receive the appropriate costs from the contracting party. In order to give the real estate economy sufficient time to adapt to the new rules, the time horizon provided by the Directive is widely used for implementation. That is, as the date of entry into force is only the 1. This appropriation is intended to cover the period from 1 December 2012 to 31 December 2012. I would ask you to give your broad support to this proposal. - Thank you.

So: The Committee on Citizens' Rights is a very lively committee, where it is being worked, and in the interests of the citizens, it is trying to achieve the best of its concerns. Thank you.

Through the many opinions we have requested – from the Ministry of Agriculture, the Ministry of Health, but also from the Animal Health Commission – I believe we have achieved that the rigid negotiating positions have been softened and have finally come to a solution that is pleasant for the farmers who are pleasant for the animalists – so, it is not pleasant for both sides, but it is portable for both parties. A solution has been found to ensure that there is no further dispute. The following was the animal welfare training and behavioural training of dogs. Here too, a problem has been brought to light, namely, that in Austria there are a lot of people who get a commercial license and say they are dog trainers, and who, without having any training without ever leading a dog, are released to the animals. A regulation has now been adopted to ensure that there is a voluntary opportunity to make an animal protection-qualified dog trainer test. This also guarantees the citizens of Austria that they do not have scharlatans, because those who take this exam really have to understand something. This was also an issue which was taken up by the Committee on Petitions and which was positively assessed by the Ministry of Health and also by other ministries as well as by the Committee on Animal Health, and an appropriate regulation was finally adopted. This shows that the Committee on Petitions is also doing something. It is not true that if we bring something to a conclusion here, nothing happens that this will be scraped off, but we end things that we have changed by law and thus implemented in the sense of the population.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-03-29-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00150_d8e17543	I call on Agenda items 12 to 14, on which the debate is being held under one. There was no oral reporting. The debate opens up to Mr Grossruck. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-03-29-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00150_d8e17570	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) High house! We are now dealing with three issues on these agenda items. I would, however, particularly like to address the first point that concerns the disappearance of people. When you hear this for the first time, you don't think there's something like that, but I've been dealing with it a little bit and I've also picked out numbers and have to say: It's horrendous, it's terrible what's happening around the world! Today we are dealing with the International Convention on the Protection of All People from Disappearing. As early as 2007, this agreement was agreed by the Federal Government and signed by the President of the Federal Republic of Germany. A total of 88 countries have so far signed it, 25 ratified, and Austria is now expected to adopt the ratification of this Convention in the National Council, in Parliament. It is a logical consequence, after we also have the seat of the Human Rights Council in Geneva, that we are at the forefront here. I know that in addition to the persecution of Christians worldwide, especially as regards human rights, it is also a major concern to the Secretary of State, and that this concern is well lifted in his hands. This Convention is divided into a Preamble and three parts, with Part 1 dealing with the actual obligations and the protection from disappearance, Part 2 contains procedural provisions for international monitoring of the provisions and Part 3 contains the usual final provisions. The definition of disappearance is also interesting. The term “disappearation” means the arrest, detention, kidnapping or any other form of withdrawal by state agents or by a person or group of persons acting with the permission, support or tolerance of the State, followed by a refusal to confirm the withdrawal of liberty, or by a concealment of the fate or the place of residence of the person who has disappeared, which the data subject withdraws from any legal protection. According to statistics of the United Nations has disappeared under this title in about 30,000 to 50,000 people over the last ten years. You have to imagine this: In more than half of the United Nations member states! That means that this is not concentrated on a country, but more than half of the countries have such cases. Above all, we know: It used to be going on in the dictatorships in South America and would make people disappear, murder people, or simply destroy their identity. I believe that this agreement, whose ratification we are adopting here today, is a very essential sign in the world and also in the state community, that this cannot be tolerated. I hope – and it is in the interest of everyone – that as many countries as possible ratify this agreement. This problem is also enormous in Mexico. There, in the last five years, 3,000 to 5,000 people have been disappeared, as a UN expert committee, who was there, has found. The government has now agreed to investigate these cases on the basis of pressure and public debate. That is to say, this commitment, this decision-making is not an empty structure, but it is, I think, a means of pressure on governments and regimes to stick more closely to human rights, to respect human rights more effectively – a long way, probably a painful way for many who are affected, but I believe, a necessary and a right one. I assume that we are now gaining unanimity in the decision-making process. - Thank you.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-03-29-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00150_d8e17635	Next speaker: Mrs Mag. Worm. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-03-29-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00150_d8e17656	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! I would like to follow closely what Mr Grossruck, Member of the European Parliament, has started on the Convention on the Disappearance of Persons. It is also important to mention that within the framework of this Convention there is also the possibility of optional complaint for victims of missing persons who are being sought, that one can contact the control body to be set up in the form of a committee, so that individual cases can be referred to this Commission. It is also possible to file state complaints to ensure that this human rights violation of the disappearance of human rights experiences the corresponding concern in the international context. What is this agreement about? . It is above all a strengthening of human rights protection within the international community which is here. will be decided, in force since 2010, ratified by 25 countries. It is also important that we now decide and ratify this agreement, because the so-called war against terrorism has put in place measures that were often overwhelming, of which it was not possible to imagine that this is still happening in 2005, 2006, 2007, 2008, 2010, 2012. As a member of the Council of Europe, I can still recall the report of a Member of the Swiss Parliament, Dick Marty, who pointed out that there should be secret prisons in Europe. He suffered many repressions due to this discovery, it was not so easy to bring light into the darkness here. Or can you remember the horrific pictures that came to us from the Iraqi prison Abu Ghraib – the perpetrator Lynndie England is still not prepared to apologise for the human rights violations and atrocities committed there – or for Guantánamo, whose closure the current President had promised? Therefore, in accordance with this Convention, secret prisons are not only no longer to be built, but also to ensure that there is no longer any need for them at all. The transfer of the various prisoners to other countries is to be prohibited. I have also come to know, like Mr Großruck, and I have seen a lot of cases – whether by Amnesty International or by other organisations – of affected persons, of victims, of their families, of how important it is for us to fight this blatant violation of human rights in the international context.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-03-29-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00150_d8e17704	President. — I call the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2012-03-29-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00150_d8e17726	Mr President. High house! There are three proposals under this item on the agenda. I can allow myself to take a brief look at all three proposals in my contribution to the debate. First of all, to the International Convention on the Protection of All Persons from Disappearing, which was already the subject of two speeches: I think the ratification of this Convention is an important step in the area of human rights. This Convention is the first binding document to understand the human rights violations occurring in connection with the disappearance as a whole phenomenon. The Convention serves to combat the practice of detention in secret places, for example in secret prisons, both preventive and repressive. The Government template provides for the submission of Austria under all the control mechanisms in this Convention, in particular an optional individual complaint and an optional state complaint. For the second time – this is the Hague Convention on Certification – it must be noted from our point of view that the accession of Uzbekistan to this Convention would remove the possibility of a control by Austrian authorities of Uzbek documents. That is to say, the Austrian opposition to the accession to which it is today protects Austrian citizens and Austrian companies from false Uzbek documents. However, it is important to stress in this context that the normal certification of Uzbek documents is of course still possible as before. Apart from Austria, Germany, Belgium, Greece and others have already objected to Uzbekistan's accession. The third presentation is about the office agreement for the International Anti-Corruption Academy (IACA). With this Headquarters Agreement, the IACA receives the same rights as the other comparable international organisations, which are based here in Austria. This agreement was a prerequisite for the IACA to settle here in Austria. The settlement of the IACA strengthens the headquarters and the Austrian profile in the field of international anti-corruption. The Austrian economy also benefits from this settlement by re-route profitability. Finally: No costs are incurred for Austria under the Office's Agreement. It is theoretically waived of tax revenue, but this loss of tax is purely fictitious, because no tax revenue would occur if the IACA were to settle outside Austria. - Thank you.

In this sense: Thank you to the Federal Government for being late at the age of five, but the ratification is coming! We will be very happy to support them. – Thank you for your attention.

I'd like to remind you briefly of what it's all about. It is based on efforts at EU level to better control arms trafficking, both illegal arms trafficking and human rights violations committed very massively with weapons, now at global level, at international level, to prevent the creation of a treaty. Negotiations are under way at UN level in April, at the United Nations level, in order to better control arms trafficking. We have wished for our green request and called for the mandatory requirement of human rights approval criteria, says that a arms deal, if it is assumed that human rights violations can happen with these weapons, is imperative to reject and prevent. We are concerned with a clear scope of application, that all weapons and ammunition, both military and security personnel, are included, that the state-to-state transfers, i.e. when a state sells weapons to another state, are regulated by the arms trade agreement, that there must be a final consumer certificate. This means that even before a arms deal is approved, it must be established who is the final consumer in order to prevent human rights violations as far as possible. And the reports on approved arms transfers must be public. This would be a very important control that allows civil society – and, hopefully, politics – to control better, and allows it to prevent wrong decisions. Last but not least, of course, we are also dealing with effective sanctions which must have a deterrent effect so that the Treaty is not broken, for example by illegal arms trafficking. I would like to stress once again that the outcome of the foreign policy committee, the unanimous decision on the consistent Austrian negotiating position on this arms trade agreement, was very welcome. We would like more, beyond party borders, that, whether the request comes from an opposition or from a government group, it is seriously discussed and decided in the sense of the matter. In this case, it was possible. Parliamentaryism lives, I would say. Please, more of it! Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2012-03-29-024-XXIV-NRSITZ-00150_d8e21468	Mr President. High house! It is now on the agenda of the Court of Auditors' activity report. That is to say, it is a matter of meeting the Court of Auditors over a year. In this context, I would like to thank all of you very much for the excellent cooperation and also – as was expressed today in the debate – for the recognition of the services. The 2011 Activity Report shows that the Court of Auditors has increased its output this year, i.e. has further increased its impact and performance. In 2011, only 13 reports with 68 reports were sent to the Federal Government, to the countries 68 reports with 74 reports, to the municipalities and municipal associations – briefly addressed – 47 reports with 13 reports. At the same time, the Court of Auditors has also respected its role as Federal Government institutions: It has carried out 27 cross-sectional tests; with the approach of comparing the performance of tasks, showing best practices or developing benchmarks. Mr Gaßner also referred to the demand procedure carried out by the Court of Auditors. When a report is published – for example in 2011 – it is asked whether the recommendations have been implemented in 2012 and a year later a follow-up review will be carried out and checked. This is the way to control effectiveness. In this context, I would like to mention that the Court of Auditors asked for 1,755 recommendations from the audited bodies, which were issued by the Court of Auditors in 2010. And this demand has shown – and that has already been mentioned – that the Court of Auditors' audits produce effects. A total of 81.8 percent of the recommendations are effected, of which 81.4 percent are federal government, 82 percent of countries and 83 percent of municipalities. If you look at the report that has been discussed today, the 2010/4 Federation, I would like to mention that this report also reflects it. If you look at the reports travel fees regulations, Statistics Austria, Embassy Budapest, it has been shown that recommendations will be implemented. The audit IT project Human Resources Management-NEEU also shows that eight of the eight recommendations have been implemented. However, there is still a need for action in the areas covered by this report, such as financial procuratorship. The examination of the use of assistance, which was also mentioned, is a good example of the fact that, when there is criticism of the Court of Auditors' audits, the Court of Auditors' recommendations are largely taken into account. This has been shown in the report on the use of assistance, where, after initial criticism, the Court of Auditors' recommendations have been implemented and the representatives of the State Defense Agency, based on the Court of Auditors' report, have also expressed their support for the end of the assistance. Unfortunately – and this is also to be mentioned – despite this very high level of implementation, especially in those areas – that was also mentioned by the Members – where system changes, changes in competence or the interaction of several stakeholders are required, the recommendations are not implemented. But their implementation would be necessary in order to ensure financial sustainability. The Court of Auditors also presents issues of vital importance in this activity report. He raises questions about what the transparency of management contracts looks like: You mentioned this today in the context of the debate. I would just like to point out: 90 percent of public enterprises do not publish the management fees, although the OECD and also the Austrian Corporate Governance Code would recommend to publish these references in terms of transparency, in terms of accountability. In addition, this activity report also deals with the question of transparency through public financial control – party law, incompatibility law, corruption prevention – where the Court of Auditors has a relatively notable function but cannot examine. It deals with the subject in a timely manner rather than accompanying control. It dealt with the issue of European Stability Mechanism, where it was shown that the control mechanisms for the ESM were not sufficient. Measures have now been taken – not least on the initiative of the European Courts of Auditors. The report also points out that the Court of Auditors is an international player by launching an initiative as General Secretariat by the Court of Auditors, which led to the adoption of a resolution in December of last year of the UN General Assembly, based on Austria, with massive support from the Foreign Office, which will also strengthen control worldwide in the future. In this context, it is also worth mentioning - as the Court of Auditors has referred to it by Mr Kogler - that there is a reporting procedure in the field of the publication of audits of legal professional representations, which is not sufficiently transparent. It would be appropriate to harmonise the reporting procedures in these procedures in the field of legal professional representations. The Court of Auditors will make its contribution by making this report available for download on the Court of Auditors’ website, after being published by the chairman of the legislative body of the legal professional representation of the report. The corresponding information on the day of delivery will also be sent to you. The Court of Auditors is currently carrying out a cross-sectional review in the area of the municipalities, which has also been referred to by the honourable Members, involving eight municipalities with more than 10 000 inhabitants. The result will be in autumn 2012. In addition, the Court of Auditors has developed community monitoring for all Austrian municipalities on the basis of publicly available data. This includes eight thematic priorities, such as risk potential, debt/indebtedness, financial situation of the municipalities, transfer intensity or administrative burden. The result of this monitoring is assessed by the municipalities in a benchmark comparison with regard to their importance. The Court of Auditors, of course, - and this has also been announced - makes this information available to all municipalities free of charge in accordance with its advisory approach. An appointment on this subject, concerning the announcement, has also been agreed with the President of the Federal Republic of Germany of Mödlhammer. The Court of Auditors thus continues its tradition of providing advice on its know-how. In this context, I would also like to mention that the Court of Auditors has contributed to the preparation of the new Community financial business directive and has also gained its know-how there. The Court of Auditors has also pointed out in several audits, such as taxation of local authorities in terms of financial compensation, fiscal policy, budgetary structure of the countries, that the current accounting system of local authorities is not sufficient to represent the true financial situation of the local authorities. The actual debt differs substantially from the debt recorded in the accounting system and in accordance with ESA. A short example: The municipalities issue Maastricht debt of €7.9 billion. The debt according to invoices is € 11.7 billion. In addition, unknown debts are still placed in outsourced legal entities. The camera accounting, namely cash accounting, leads to the recording of cash flows from asset disposals, but does not cover the reduction of assets. For this reason, there are always financing models in the area of communities which affect transparency. In February 2012, the Court of Auditors was made aware of a model of the financing of public authorities by a representative of the Court of Auditors of this House, not by municipal mandates, but by a representative of the Court of Auditors, and faced with the question of whether the Court of Auditors already had knowledge and assessment in this regard and that tentway was not an individual case. On the basis of its audit findings, the Court of Auditors, in accordance with its advisory approach, has had a first-level review on the need to ensure the sustainable security of the finances of the municipalities. It is worth mentioning, Mrs Lapp, that this model is, among other things, distributed and promoted by the former head of the cabinet of the Federal Minister for Foreign Affairs, Mr Schmid, or by the former head of the cabinet of the Federal Minister for Foresting. With this indication one should see who among others is behind these models or who is interested in them. Recalling ESA demarcations that debt is shifted, burdens are shifted into the future, that the objective of the Stability Pact as regards the establishment of upper limits of liability and, at the same time, that the economic benefits in terms of interest should be very critically assessed, the Court of Auditors consulted the municipality supervisor Steiermark and asked to deliver an opinion on this matter. A copy of this letter has also been sent to the Mayor of the municipality of Tentweg and to the President of the Court of Auditors of this House, who has addressed this model with the request for information to the Court of Auditors. The Court of Auditors sees it as its constitutional responsibility to contribute, on the basis of its audit findings, to the sustainable reorganisation of the state budget of all local authorities, in particular on transparency and accountability and, in this context, to advise the local authorities. An understanding of tasks, as I know from discussions with you, which you all agree with and which is also borne by you all. In this connection, after the President-in-Office of the Council is here, I would also like to mention that the Court of Auditors is currently carrying out an examination of the completed phase of Parliament's reorganisation. At the same time, it was agreed that a second review will be carried out in 2014 following the completion of the pre-design plans. The Court of Auditors is also showing that, in accordance with international standards, it is taking up the possibility of timely examination while at the same time exercising its responsibility. The Court of Auditors has therefore shown that it has been able to significantly increase its effects and benefits on lower inputs. This was only possible through the cooperation and support and, in particular, the excellent performance of the staff of the Court of Auditors, for which I also thank the employees at this point. [[General condolences.]] The Court of Auditors has also acted anti-cyclically in recent years and has done everything to make reserves so that it can continue to ensure that tasks are carried out with reduced resources in the future. Therefore, through rationalizations, at the same time through redimensional and temporary delay of projects, it was possible to reach the 31st century. December 2011 to save €6.4 million in reserves, so that the Court of Auditors, among other things, can also pay the construction costs to the BIG. Due to the organisational reform, clustering of audit agents and at the same time reducing internal performance, it was possible to cut the planning sites from 345 planning points in 2004 to 326 planning points in 2012. The Court of Auditors’ staff – and I think that is unique – increased by €263,000 from 2009 to 2011. Compared with the Supreme Institutions, the Court of Auditors showed the slightest increase in expenditure or expenditure by 2011. Despite this, the Court of Auditors is aware of its responsibility and has therefore also agreed that its planning sites will be further reduced from 326 planning points to 322 planning posts in 2014. The Court of Auditors is happy to make its contribution to the pact adopted yesterday and to take further action in its field. However, the Federal Financial Framework Act 2013 to 2016, adopted by the Council of Ministers, sees no increase in the budget for the Court of Auditors but, taking into account the additional costs of the service providers' contributions, a further actual reduction in the budget. the amount of € 7 664 000. Due to the forward-looking, extremely economical approach to the budget in recent years, the Court of Auditors can make a financial contribution of more than €4.6 million from its own initiative. However, it is not possible to carry out the full budget reduction, in particular in 2015 and 2016, while at the same time performing the constitutional tasks fully. As President of the Court of Auditors, it is my duty – even if it is a little later now – to make these figures and facts available to you as a basis of decision in the context of the decision-making of the budget. There is no doubt that a budget reduction beyond the Court of Auditors' possible contribution will also have an impact on the control and thus also on your sovereignty of control. In this context, I would like to thank all the members of the Committee on the Court of Auditors who have held a very constructive debate on this subject and who have also intended to table an amendment. Unfortunately, this amendment did not find the necessary support yesterday. However, the Court of Auditors will now do everything in the context of the limited financial resources available, as in the past, so that the funds are used optimally in the interests of citizens. – I thank you once again for your cooperation.

The Court of Auditors points out in two points that we have already mounted several times, on the one hand, in the absence of testing opportunities for companies under 50 percent participation of the public sector and on the other hand in municipalities of less than 10 000 inhabitants. Now, of course, we would like to see a better overview of public finances and more transparency, that these competences should be extended. Only yesterday's decision of the Federal Financial Framework Act did the Court of Auditors be severely cut down in its funds, what we have decided to fight, and, I say, it's not every day evening, it will continue to fight. We will continue to work to ensure that the Court of Auditors is provided with the financial resources it needs in order to fulfil its constitutional obligations and to be granted to it.
